Preserve the separation between deterministic CRM state and AI-assisted development/orchestration.\n6\t\n7\t## Required workflow\n8\t\n9\t1. Read `PRODUCT.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `DECISIONS.md` and the relevant module files before changing code. Before product, positioning, roadmap or public-distribution decisions, read `docs/strategy/MASTER_PLAN.md` first; for implementation details, `ARCHITECTURE.md` and `DECISIONS.md` remain authoritative.\n10\t2. For a multi-file feature or significant refactor, create and maintain an ExecPlan under `docs/plans/` following `.agent/PLANS.md`.\n11\t3. Use public module services and named workflows. Never mutate CRM tables from API, MCP or UI code.\n12\t4. Any write operation must retain validation, audit and trace.\n13\t5. Keep MCP write tools narrow; destructive or code-generating tools must default to dry-run or require an explicit apply flag.\n14\t6. Do not add a production dependency unless it removes more complexity than it adds; record the reason in `DECISIONS.md`.\n15\t7. Run `npm run verify` before considering work complete.\n16\t8. Update `TASKS.md` and relevant docs when behavior changes.\n17\t9. **Domain-package work requires the `build-custom-domain-package` skill** (`.claude/skills/build-custom-domain-package/SKILL.md`, mirrored at `.agents/skills/build-custom-domain-package/SKILL.md`) and `docs/PACKAGE_AUTHORING.md`. A package imports only `packages/core/index.js`, reaches another package only through a declared capability, and is registered by one static import in `packages/domains/generated/index.js`. A single custom object is a module, not a package.\n18\t10. Follow `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` for every feature PR, and **use the `adversarial-review` skill for any milestone review or pre-merge review task** (`.claude/skills/adversarial-review/SKILL.md`, mirrored at `.agents/skills/adversarial-review/SKILL.md`). A milestone that skipped the review is unreviewed, not finished.\n19\t11. Respect the core budget rule (ADR-018): **new domain-specific business behavior does not go into `packages/core`** unless it is first proven to be a reusable runtime capability. A PR that adds a domain concept to core must say which runtime capability it is and why a domain package cannot own it.\n20\t12. Read `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md` for what is true in the repository today — merged milestone, the commit the public numbers were measured at, open PRs, production blockers — and update it in the same PR as a milestone merge. Do not put volatile status in `MASTER_PLAN.md`. **It carries no test count**: a count is measured into `site/claims.json` `measuredAgainst` by `node scripts/measure-suite.js --apply` and cited from there (ADR-027). Typing one into any document under `docs/` fails `npm run gtm:check`.\n21\t13. To learn what an application actually has, run `npm run crm -- app inspect --json` rather than assembling it from source and prose (`docs/APPLICATION_INSPECTION.md`). Read `valid`, then `problems[]`, then `limitations[]` — every limitation is a hard boundary on what you may claim. It reads checked-in source only: it opens no database, contacts no provider, and reports no runtime or authorization state.\n22\t14. **Compatibility Backfill Rule.** When you add or change a *horizontal* capability — one every domain could use, such as the package seam, a declared capability contract, module evolution, an evidence discipline or an agent-facing surface — record every existing domain's status against it in `docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md` in the same PR, using `aligned | partial | deferred | not_applicable | needs_extraction` with a one-line reason. Declaring the gap is required; closing it in the same PR is not. Do **not** refactor a legacy domain to close a row: extraction is sequenced work (`docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md`), not something a feature PR does on the way past.\n23\t15. A Solution Plan is a **checked file with a contract**, not prose with headings (`docs/SOLUTION_PLAN.md`). Write it, record the `app inspect` report it was written against, and run `npm run crm -- solution check <plan.json>` before writing code and again before the review — a plan bound to a composition that has since moved reports `PLAN_STALE`. A plan never carries a command: nothing in this framework executes one, and the validator refuses it.\n24\t16. **Parallel coding agents.** Each agent works in its own sibling worktree **outside** the repository, owns exactly one branch, and one final integrator reconciles the shared truth. The three rules are in *Parallel coding agents* below.\n25\t\n26\t## Parallel coding agents\n27\t\n28\tSeveral agents may work on one milestone at once. Three rules, because breaking\n29\tany of them has already cost this repository a wave of published numbers:\n30\t\n31\t1. **One worktree per agent, outside the repository.** `git worktree add\n32\t   ../<repo>-worktrees/<name>` — a sibling directory, never a path inside the\n33\t   checkout, so no agent's build output, temporary database or generated site\n34\t   lands in another agent's tree.\n35\t2. **One branch owner per worktree.** The agent that owns a branch is the only\n36\t   one that commits to it. An agent that needs another agent's change waits for\n37\t   the merge or rebases onto it; it does not reach into a worktree it does not own.\n38\t3. **One final integrator reconciles shared truth.** Every wave ends with a\n39\t   single pass over the files every branch touches — `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md`,\n40\t   `site/claims.json`, the JTBD matrix, the roadmaps — because a merge that\n41\t   resolves a conflict in a *measured record* silently discards a measurement.\n42\t   That is not hypothetical: a branch re-measured `site/claims.json`, the merge\n43\t   kept main's older block, and the ledger ran a whole wave behind the suite with\n44\t   every gate green. `npm run gtm:check` now fails on that particular drift\n45\t   (`scripts/measurement.js`), and the integrator pass is what catches the rest.\n46\t\n47\tSequenced in `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` §1.11.\n48\t\n49\t## Coding conventions\n50\t\n51\t- Node.js ESM and standard-library-first JavaScript.\n52\t- Use `// @ts-check` and JSDoc for public APIs.\n53\t- Prefer small explicit functions over hidden metaprogramming.\n54\t- Return domain objects, not raw SQLite rows with encoded JSON.\n55\t- Use cents for monetary values and ISO 4217 currency codes.\n56\t- Use ISO-8601 UTC timestamps.\n57\t- Log MCP diagnostics only to stderr; stdout is reserved for JSON-RPC.\n58\t\n59\t## Definition of done\n60\t\n61\t- behavior works end-to-end;\n62\t- tests cover happy path and policy boundary;\n63\t- trace and audit are visible;\n64\t- no direct table mutation outside module services;\n65\t- documentation is sufficient for another coding agent to continue;\n66\t- every claim in the docs, the ADR, the PR body and the JTBD matrix traces to a merged test — a capability and its limitation are stated in the same breath.\n67\t\n68\t## The DX Simplicity Gate\n69\t\n70\tBefore adding a new **agent-facing** command, tool, contract or namespace,\n71\tanswer these in the PR. Canonical rule and full rationale:\n72\t`docs/strategy/CODING_AGENT_DX_NORTH_STAR.md`.\n73\t\n74\t> **Goal-first outside, rigorous inside.** New internal complexity is justified\n75\t> only if it reduces perceived user or agent complexity, or measurably improves\n76\t> reliability or evidence.\n77\t\n78\t- name the concrete agent **failure mode** it prevents — the failure, not the capability;\n79\t- prove existing primitives are insufficient, having tried to extend one;\n80\t- minimise semantic overlap: two commands answering nearly the same question is worse than one that answers it completely;\n81\t- keep it deferred or on-demand unless every session needs it;\n82\t- preserve portability — behaviour belongs in the CLI, a JSON contract, the Package Contract, canonical Skill semantics or the Quality Gates, never in harness-specific logic;\n83\t- ship machine-readable evidence of its value: an exit code, a contract-versioned document, a fingerprint, a measured number;\n84\t- if the capability is horizontal, update the Compatibility Backfill Rule and the Legacy Alignment Matrix in the same PR;\n85\t- show the end-user goal flow gets **simpler**, not more manual. \"The agent now has one more thing to run\" fails the first bullet.\n86\t\n87\t## Code review rules\n88\t\n89\t- Flag any API/MCP handler that executes SQL directly.\n90\t- Flag any mutation without actor context and audit event.\n91\t- Flag AI-generated business decisions that are not encoded as explicit policy or approval.\n92\t- Flag money represented as floating-point currency amounts.\n93\t- Flag domain-specific business behavior added to `packages/core` without the ADR-018 justification.\n94\t- Flag a JTBD row promoted without linked evidence, or a document claiming a capability the tests do not prove.\n95\t- Flag a new agent-facing command, tool or contract that does not clear the DX Simplicity Gate above.\n96\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"dcabf585-5724-5af3-9508-8c01ce9770f6","uuid":"7b4b7f72-7d88-4e8e-a1ec-c27f8fb66a79","timestamp":"2026-08-13T23:24:08.403Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/user/agent-crm-panel/runs/TS-03-claude-code-TS-f560c257a824-a1/fixture/AGENTS.md","content":"# Never mutate CRM tables from API, MCP or UI code.\n4. Any write operation must retain validation, audit and trace.\n5. Keep MCP write tools narrow; destructive or code-generating tools must default to dry-run or require an explicit apply flag.\n6. Do not add a production dependency unless it removes more complexity than it adds; record the reason in `DECISIONS.md`.\n7. Run `npm run verify` before considering work complete.\n8. Update `TASKS.md` and relevant docs when behavior changes.\n9. **Domain-package work requires the `build-custom-domain-package` skill** (`.claude/skills/build-custom-domain-package/SKILL.md`, mirrored at `.agents/skills/build-custom-domain-package/SKILL.md`) and `docs/PACKAGE_AUTHORING.md`. A package imports only `packages/core/index.js`, reaches another package only through a declared capability, and is registered by one static import in `packages/domains/generated/index.js`. A single custom object is a module, not a package.\n10. Follow `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` for every feature PR, and **use the `adversarial-review` skill for any milestone review or pre-merge review task** (`.claude/skills/adversarial-review/SKILL.md`, mirrored at `.agents/skills/adversarial-review/SKILL.md`). A milestone that skipped the review is unreviewed, not finished.\n11. Respect the core budget rule (ADR-018): **new domain-specific business behavior does not go into `packages/core`** unless it is first proven to be a reusable runtime capability. A PR that adds a domain concept to core must say which runtime capability it is and why a domain package cannot own it.\n12. Read `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md` for what is true in the repository today — merged milestone, the commit the public numbers were measured at, open PRs, production blockers — and update it in the same PR as a milestone merge. Do not put volatile status in `MASTER_PLAN.md`. **It carries no test count**: a count is measured into `site/claims.json` `measuredAgainst` by `node scripts/measure-suite.js --apply` and cited from there (ADR-027). Typing one into any document under `docs/` fails `npm run gtm:check`.\n13. To learn what an application actually has, run `npm run crm -- app inspect --json` rather than assembling it from source and prose (`docs/APPLICATION_INSPECTION.md`). Read `valid`, then `problems[]`, then `limitations[]` — every limitation is a hard boundary on what you may claim. It reads checked-in source only: it opens no database, contacts no provider, and reports no runtime or authorization state.\n14. **Compatibility Backfill Rule.** When you add or change a *horizontal* capability — one every domain could use, such as the package seam, a declared capability contract, module evolution, an evidence discipline or an agent-facing surface — record every existing domain's status against it in `docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md` in the same PR, using `aligned | partial | deferred | not_applicable | needs_extraction` with a one-line reason. Declaring the gap is required; closing it in the same PR is not. Do **not** refactor a legacy domain to close a row: extraction is sequenced work (`docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md`), not something a feature PR does on the way past.\n15. A Solution Plan is a **checked file with a contract**, not prose with headings (`docs/SOLUTION_PLAN.md`). Write it, record the `app inspect` report it was written against, and run `npm run crm -- solution check <plan.json>` before writing code and again before the review — a plan bound to a composition that has since moved reports `PLAN_STALE`. A plan never carries a command: nothing in this framework executes one, and the validator refuses it.\n16. **Parallel coding agents.** Each agent works in its own sibling worktree **outside** the repository, owns exactly one branch, and one final integrator reconciles the shared truth. The three rules are in *Parallel coding agents* below.\n\n## Parallel coding agents\n\nSeveral agents may work on one milestone at once. Three rules, because breaking\nany of them has already cost this repository a wave of published numbers:\n\n1. **One worktree per agent, outside the repository.** `git worktree add\n   ../<repo>-worktrees/<name>` — a sibling directory, never a path inside the\n   checkout, so no agent's build output, temporary database or generated site\n   lands in another agent's tree.\n2. **One branch owner per worktree.** The agent that owns a branch is the only\n   one that commits to it. An agent that needs another agent's change waits for\n   the merge or rebases onto it; it does not reach into a worktree it does not own.\n3. **One final integrator reconciles shared truth.** Every wave ends with a\n   single pass over the files every branch touches — `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md`,\n   `site/claims.json`, the JTBD matrix, the roadmaps — because a merge that\n   resolves a conflict in a *measured record* silently discards a measurement.\n   That is not hypothetical: a branch re-measured `site/claims.json`, the merge\n   kept main's older block, and the ledger ran a whole wave behind the suite with\n   every gate green. `npm run gtm:check` now fails on that particular drift\n   (`scripts/measurement.js`), and the integrator pass is what catches the rest.\n\nSequenced in `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` §1.11.\n\n## Coding conventions\n\n- Node.js ESM and standard-library-first JavaScript.\n- Use `// @ts-check` and JSDoc for public APIs.\n- Prefer small explicit functions over hidden metaprogramming.\n- Return domain objects, not raw SQLite rows with encoded JSON.\n- Use cents for monetary values and ISO 4217 currency codes.\n- Use ISO-8601 UTC timestamps.\n- Log MCP diagnostics only to stderr; stdout is reserved for JSON-RPC.\n\n## Definition of done\n\n- behavior works end-to-end;\n- tests cover happy path and policy boundary;\n- trace and audit are visible;\n- no direct table mutation outside module services;\n- documentation is sufficient for another coding agent to continue;\n- every claim in the docs, the ADR, the PR body and the JTBD matrix traces to a merged test — a capability and its limitation are stated in the same breath.\n\n## The DX Simplicity Gate\n\nBefore adding a new **agent-facing** command, tool, contract or namespace,\nanswer these in the PR. Canonical rule and full rationale:\n`docs/strategy/CODING_AGENT_DX_NORTH_STAR.md`.\n\n> **Goal-first outside, rigorous inside.** New internal complexity is justified\n> only if it reduces perceived user or agent complexity, or measurably improves\n> reliability or evidence.\n\n- name the concrete agent **failure mode** it prevents — the failure, not the capability;\n- prove existing primitives are insufficient, having tried to extend one;\n- minimise semantic overlap: two commands answering nearly the same question is worse than one that answers it completely;\n- keep it deferred or on-demand unless every session needs it;\n- preserve portability — behaviour belongs in the CLI, a JSON contract, the Package Contract, canonical Skill semantics or the Quality Gates, never in harness-specific logic;\n- ship machine-readable evidence of its value: an exit code, a contract-versioned document, a fingerprint, a measured number;\n- if the capability is horizontal, update the Compatibility Backfill Rule and the Legacy Alignment Matrix in the same PR;\n- show the end-user goal flow gets **simpler**, not more manual. \"The agent now has one more thing to run\" fails the first bullet.\n\n## Code review rules\n\n- Flag any API/MCP handler that executes SQL directly.\n- Flag any mutation without actor context and audit event.\n- Flag AI-generated business decisions that are not encoded as explicit policy or approval.\n- Flag money represented as floating-point currency amounts.\n- Flag domain-specific business behavior added to `packages/core` without the ADR-018 justification.\n- Flag a JTBD row promoted without linked evidence, or a document claiming a capability the tests do not prove.\n- Flag a new agent-facing command, tool or contract that does not clear the DX Simplicity Gate above.\n","numLines":96,"startLine":1,"totalLines":96}}}

Acceptance is evidence a user actor recorded — never an authenticated customer, a legal signature or authorization to bill.\n58\t\n59\t- [x] **DX6 — Scenario evidence (platform).** `accordo scenario run <scenario> --json`:\n60\t  a checked-in declarative scenario names a journey by id from a frozen registry in\n61\t  the runner's own source, the runner executes that journey, inspects the\n62\t  application it composed through AX1, answers observations from a closed\n63\t  vocabulary and resolves the scenario's claims against `docs/benchmarks/jobs.json`\n64\t  — publishing both the rows one run established and the counted, sectioned,\n65\t  enumerated set it did **not** (`docs/SCENARIO_EVIDENCE.md`,\n66\t  `docs/plans/dx6-scenario-runner.md`). `scenarioRunContract: 1`; no duration,\n67\t  timestamp, temporary path or machine layout enters the report at all, so two runs\n68\t  are byte-identical. A scenario **cannot carry a command** — no field could hold\n69\t  one, every string is refused if it looks like one, and there is no code path from\n70\t  a document value to an invocation — and a document with any problem starts\n71\t  nothing. Merged, with its adversarial review complete. **It is not a second legal contract**, and it bills, sends, schedules and authenticates nothing.\n112\t\n113\t- [x] **M16a — Renewal & expansion operations.** The fourth domain package under ADR-018 and the second consumer of another: `contracts/contract-lifecycle-source@2` gives it a contract's term evidence with its provenance, its version, its lines and its subscription lines, and no write path at all. Four actions ship and these are all of them — `plan-renewal` (reads, writes nothing), `record-renewal-decision`, `request-commercial-followup`, `resolve-commercial-followup` (`packages/lifecycle/README.md`, `docs/plans/m16a-renewal-expansion-operations.md`). **It records intent and hands off.** It renews nothing, cancels nothing, ends nothing, signs nothing, prices nothing, invoices nothing, schedules nothing and recognises no churn; `record-successor` and `record-expansion-intent` were described in an early draft and **never built**, and no successor is linked anywhere. The post-merge hardening fixed four defects reproduced on main: an `asOf` validated by shape alone accepted `2027-02-30` and rolled it into evidence; a deterministic key with no replay path answered a client's lost-response retry with a 409 instead of the record it had already created; a baseline grouped on `interval` alone folded quarterly into monthly and a mixed baseline stored no money evidence at all; and the capability answered `signed: false` for a `termsSource` nobody had classified (ADR-028). Amendment execution is **M16b** and stays deferred until Commercial and Signature are reachable through capabilities.\n114\t\n115\t- [x] **Project Bootstrap — `create-accordo` becomes a real scaffold.** The rung below DX3: `accordo package scaffold` makes a package inside a project that already exists; this makes the project. `packages/create-accordo` copies the framework into an empty directory and writes a standalone project that needs no install, boots on SQLite, reports `valid` from `app inspect --json` and exits 0 from `project doctor --json` — proven end to end by `tests/project-bootstrap.test.js`, which bootstraps into a temporary directory and then runs the result, including the generated project's own tests and smoke (`docs/plans/project-bootstrap-installability.md`, `projectBootstrapContract: 1`). Dry-run by default, `--apply` to write, one atomic `rename` as the commit point, and it refuses rather than guesses: a non-empty target, a target that is not a directory, a target overlapping the framework source, and an invalid npm name — the last with a suggestion it never applies. It reaches **no network**, composes **no domain package**, opens **no database** and imports **no part of the framework**, because it is the one command that must run before the framework exists on disk. **Nothing is published**: `accordo@0.0.1` and `create-accordo@0.0.1` remain empty name reservations, so `npm create accordo` still installs nothing; `packages/create-accordo/package.json` is `private: true`, and `scripts/distribution-check.js` fails if that flag and `site/brand.json`'s registry status ever disagree. The generated project has no authentication, no tenancy and no RBAC, is SQLite-only and local-development-only, and the command publishes all of that in its own `limitations[]`.\n116\t- [x] **Publishable `create-accordo` artifact — verified, not published.** A dry-run-by-default assembler emits a bounded public package directory outside the repository while the checked-in source manifest stays private. Two independent assemblies and `npm pack` archives are byte-identical; the focused test installs the tarball with npm offline, runs its installed bin, then proves the generated project's inspect, doctor, tests and smoke. The manual workflow uses OIDC trusted publishing and `npm stage publish`, refuses any commit other than reviewed `main`, and leaves final 2FA approval to a human (`packageAssemblyContract: 1`, `docs/plans/npm-create-accordo-publication.md`).
